I try my best to pay attention, I really do. I don't ever want to seem rude or like I'm ignoring anybody. But, um... I guess maybe a little bit? \n\nLike, when folks are talking through things with me—you know, like ride times, or pantry lists, or plans for church—I always make sure to listen real good at first. I'm right there with 'em. But then... I don't know, my mind just sort of slips off somewhere else. And I end up missing pieces I really needed to hear. \n\nI feel terrible about it, honestly, because I really am trying to be good and listen. I hope that makes sense... did I answer your question right?\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... act on impulse? I don't want to cause any trouble or make any sudden moves that might upset anyone. \n\nI mean, maybe sometimes I'll speak up a little too quick if I'm really excited about something, but I try real hard to keep that in check. Well, I... I guess I do move around a bit, but I really try not to let it be a distraction to anybody. I know it can be annoying for other people, so I try my best to just keep it small.

Well, um... I don't work right now, I'm just focusing on taking care of things at home. But, um, to answer your question... it's kind of everywhere, to be honest. I hope it's alright to say that.

I just start feeling really, um, trapped? Like I'm stuck and I just have to get up and do something, anything, just to get that energy out. 

I really try to hold it in, especially at church, because I don't want to be disrespectful or make anyone think I'm not paying attention. I just... I can't seem to help it sometimes. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding too difficult or anything.

And I just have to get up or do something. \n\nI try really hard not to be a bother to anyone when it happens, I promise. Does that... is that the kind of thing you can help with?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to focus on things or pay attention when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I try my best to pay attention, I really do. I don't ever want to seem rude or like I'm ignoring anybody. But, um... I guess maybe a little bit? \n\nLike, when folks are talking through things with me—you know, like ride times, or pantry lists, or plans for church—I always make sure to listen real good at first. I'm right there with 'em. But then... I don't know, my mind just sort of slips off somewhere else. And I end up missing pieces I really needed to hear. \n\nI feel terrible about it, honestly, because I really am trying to be good and listen. I hope that makes sense... did I answer your question right?\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... act on impulse? Well, I don't think I do that too much. I really try to be careful and think about what I'm doing, especially around other people. I don't want to cause any trouble or make any sudden moves that might upset anyone. \n\nI mean, maybe sometimes I'll speak up a little too quick if I'm really excited about something, but I try real hard to keep that in check. I don't think I do anything too crazy without thinking. Is that... does that sound okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answers.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I guess I do move around a bit, but I really try not to let it be a distraction to anybody. I know it can be annoying for other people, so I try my best to just keep it small. \n\nLike, um... even when I'm really trying to be respectful and sit quiet, my leg just sort of starts bouncing on its own. Or my fingers will just be tapping on the arm of the chair. Sometimes I don't even realize I'm doing it until I look down and see I'm twisting a piece of paper or my shirt hem in my hands the whole time. \n\nI'm so sorry if I'm doing it right now, I really don't mean to. It's just a little habit, you know? I hope that's not too bad of a thing to admit... is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: When did you first start feeling like you have a motor running inside you?\n\nPatient: A motor? Oh, well... yeah, I guess that is a real good way to put it. I hope it's okay to agree with that. I mean, I've always had a little bit of extra energy, just trying to be helpful and keep busy, you know? But feeling like it's... well, really hard to manage? I guess it's been about two years and two months now. \n\nIt started getting a lot more noticeable right around the time my older relative's health changed. I had to step up and take on a whole lot more responsibilities, which I don't mind at all—I really want to do a good job taking care of them and making sure everything gets done right. But ever since then, that... that motor feeling just won't seem to quit, no matter how hard I try to settle down and be still. \n\nI feel bad complaining about it, because I just want to be useful to them, you know? I hope I'm explaining that right... is that the kind of answer you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this restlessness and need to move around at work or school as well, or is it mainly in situations like church or at home?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I don't work right now, I'm just focusing on taking care of things at home. But, um, to answer your question... it's kind of everywhere, to be honest. I hope it's alright to say that. \n\nLike I was saying about that motor feeling, it just goes all day. So if I'm at home, or if I have to go to the clinic with my relative, or even if I'm sitting in church... I just start feeling really, um, trapped? Like I'm stuck and I just have to get up and do something, anything, just to get that energy out. \n\nI really try to hold it in, especially at church, because I don't want to be disrespectful or make anyone think I'm not paying attention. Well, um... I really try hard to make sure my pacing and fidgeting doesn't bother anyone. I just want to be useful. But... I guess, if I'm being completely honest—and I hope it's okay to tell you this—it has caused some trouble. I feel so ashamed even saying it out loud.

I guess I do move around a bit, but I really try not to let it be a distraction to anybody. I know it can be annoying for other people, so I try my best to just keep it small. \n\nLike, um... even when I'm really trying to be respectful and sit quiet, my leg just sort of starts bouncing on its own. Sometimes I don't even realize I'm doing it until I look down and see I'm twisting a piece of paper or my shirt hem in my hands the whole time. \n\nI'm so sorry if I'm doing it right now, I really don't mean to. It's just a little habit, you know? I hope that's not too bad of a thing to admit... is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: When did you first start feeling like you have a motor running inside you?\n\nPatient: A motor? I mean, I've always had a little bit of extra energy, just trying to be helpful and keep busy, you know? But feeling like it's... well, really hard to manage? I guess it's been about two years and two months now. \n\nIt started getting a lot more noticeable right around the time my older relative's health changed. I had to step up and take on a whole lot more responsibilities, which I don't mind at all—I really want to do a good job taking care of them and making sure everything gets done right. Well, um... I really do try my best to pay attention, I promise I do. I want to make sure I get everything exactly right for everybody. But... well, I guess sometimes things do slip a little bit. I hope it's okay to admit that.

I don't want to cause any trouble or make any sudden moves that might upset anyone. \n\nI mean, maybe sometimes I'll speak up a little too quick if I'm really excited about something, but I try real hard to keep that in check. I don't think I do anything too crazy without thinking. Is that... does that sound okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answers.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I guess I do move around a bit, but I really try not to let it be a distraction to anybody. I know it can be annoying for other people, so I try my best to just keep it small. \n\nLike, um... even when I'm really trying to be respectful and sit quiet, my leg just sort of starts bouncing on its own. Or my fingers will just be tapping on the arm of the chair. Sometimes I don't even realize I'm doing it until I look down and see I'm twisting a piece of paper or my shirt hem in my hands the whole time. \n\nI'm so sorry if I'm doing it right now, I really don't mean to. It's just a little habit, you know? I hope that's not too bad of a thing to admit... is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: When did you first start feeling like you have a motor running inside you?\n\nPatient: A motor? Oh, well... yeah, I guess that is a real good way to put it. I hope it's okay to agree with that. I mean, I've always had a little bit of extra energy, just trying to be helpful and keep busy, you know? But feeling like it's... well, really hard to manage? I guess it's been about two years and two months now. \n\nIt started getting a lot more noticeable right around the time my older relative's health changed. I had to step up and take on a whole lot more responsibilities, which I don't mind at all—I really want to do a good job taking care of them and making sure everything gets done right. But ever since then, that... that motor feeling just won't seem to quit, no matter how hard I try to settle down and be still. \n\nI feel bad complaining about it, because I just want to be useful to them, you know? I hope I'm explaining that right... is that the kind of answer you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this restlessness and need to move around at work or school as well, or is it mainly in situations like church or at home?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I don't work right now, I'm just focusing on taking care of things at home. But, um, to answer your question... it's kind of everywhere, to be honest. I hope it's alright to say that. \n\nLike I was saying about that motor feeling, it just goes all day. So if I'm at home, or if I have to go to the clinic with my relative, or even if I'm sitting in church... I just start feeling really, um, trapped? Like I'm stuck and I just have to get up and do something, anything, just to get that energy out. \n\nI really try to hold it in, especially at church, because I don't want to be disrespectful or make anyone think I'm not paying attention. I just... I can't seem to help it sometimes. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding too difficult or anything.\n\nDoctor: Has this feeling of having a motor running inside you ever caused problems for you in your daily activities or relationships?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try hard to make sure my pacing and fidgeting doesn't bother anyone. I just want to be useful. But... I guess, if I'm being completely honest—and I hope it's okay to tell you this—it has caused some trouble. I feel so ashamed even saying it out loud. \n\nUm... the people at church, and down at the food pantry, and even my own family... they've sort of stopped trusting me with things. Like, they don't ask me to do rides anymore, or help keep track of the appointments, or handle the supplies. I just get too antsy and moving too fast, and things get messed up. \n\nI feel like I just keep letting everybody down, even when I really, truly mean to help them. I'm trying so hard to do better, I promise I am. Well, um... I really try to be polite, I promise. I know it's bad manners to talk over folks, and I just want to show everyone I'm ready to help. But... well, I guess if I'm being completely honest... I do kind of do that sometimes. I hope you don't think I'm a rude person.

I know it can be annoying for other people, so I try my best to just keep it small. \n\nLike, um... even when I'm really trying to be respectful and sit quiet, my leg just sort of starts bouncing on its own. Or my fingers will just be tapping on the arm of the chair. Sometimes I don't even realize I'm doing it until I look down and see I'm twisting a piece of paper or my shirt hem in my hands the whole time. \n\nI'm so sorry if I'm doing it right now, I really don't mean to. It's just a little habit, you know? I hope that's not too bad of a thing to admit... is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: When did you first start feeling like you have a motor running inside you?\n\nPatient: A motor? Oh, well... yeah, I guess that is a real good way to put it. I hope it's okay to agree with that. I mean, I've always had a little bit of extra energy, just trying to be helpful and keep busy, you know? But feeling like it's... well, really hard to manage? I guess it's been about two years and two months now. \n\nIt started getting a lot more noticeable right around the time my older relative's health changed. I had to step up and take on a whole lot more responsibilities, which I don't mind at all—I really want to do a good job taking care of them and making sure everything gets done right. But ever since then, that... that motor feeling just won't seem to quit, no matter how hard I try to settle down and be still. \n\nI feel bad complaining about it, because I just want to be useful to them, you know? I hope I'm explaining that right... is that the kind of answer you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this restlessness and need to move around at work or school as well, or is it mainly in situations like church or at home?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I don't work right now, I'm just focusing on taking care of things at home. But, um, to answer your question... it's kind of everywhere, to be honest. I hope it's alright to say that. \n\nLike I was saying about that motor feeling, it just goes all day. So if I'm at home, or if I have to go to the clinic with my relative, or even if I'm sitting in church... I just start feeling really, um, trapped? Like I'm stuck and I just have to get up and do something, anything, just to get that energy out. \n\nI really try to hold it in, especially at church, because I don't want to be disrespectful or make anyone think I'm not paying attention. I just... I can't seem to help it sometimes. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding too difficult or anything.\n\nDoctor: Has this feeling of having a motor running inside you ever caused problems for you in your daily activities or relationships?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try hard to make sure my pacing and fidgeting doesn't bother anyone. I just want to be useful. But... I guess, if I'm being completely honest—and I hope it's okay to tell you this—it has caused some trouble. I feel so ashamed even saying it out loud. \n\nUm... the people at church, and down at the food pantry, and even my own family... they've sort of stopped trusting me with things. Like, they don't ask me to do rides anymore, or help keep track of the appointments, or handle the supplies. I just get too antsy and moving too fast, and things get messed up. \n\nI feel like I just keep letting everybody down, even when I really, truly mean to help them. I'm trying so hard to do better, I promise I am. I hope that doesn't make me sound like a bad person... did I answer your question okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble paying attention or following instructions at home, like when you're trying to take care of your relative or doing chores?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really do try my best to pay attention, I promise I do. I want to make sure I get everything exactly right for everybody. But... well, I guess sometimes things do slip a little bit. I hope it's okay to admit that. \n\nLike, when folks are talking through the ride times for my relative, or going over the pantry lists, or explaining the plans for church... I listen real good at first. I'm right there with them. But then, it's like... my mind just sort of slips off somewhere else, even though I don't want it to. And then I end up missing pieces of what they said, pieces I really needed to hear to do a good job. \n\nI feel awful when it happens, because I know they're counting on me. I really am trying to follow the instructions better. Does that... does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding too scattered.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're speaking?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try to be polite, I promise. I know it's bad manners to talk over folks, and I just want to show everyone I'm ready to help. But... well, I guess if I'm being completely honest... I do kind of do that sometimes. I hope you don't think I'm a rude person. \n\nLike, if the pastor, or my cousin, or the lady down at the food pantry is explaining something to me... I just get so eager, and I jump right in with an answer before they're even done talking. My brain just goes so fast, and the words just spill out before I can stop them. \n\nAnd then... well, then I realize I answered the completely wrong thing, or I see that I made them mad because I cut them off. I feel so awful when it happens, because I really don't mean any disrespect by it. I'm just trying to show them I'm on top of things. I hope I'm not making myself sound too terrible... did I explain that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you often blurting out answers before the questions have been completely asked?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I know I just mentioned jumping in sometimes when folks are talking, but... I don't think I really blurt out answers to questions before they're finished asking. At least, I really hope I haven't been doing that. \n\nI try so, so hard to be respectful when someone is directly asking me something, because I want to make sure I hear the whole thing so I can actually be helpful to them. I haven't noticed myself doing that, anyway. I hope that's a good thing... am I making sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mind wandering and trouble paying attention are more problematic for you than your restlessness and need to move around?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really hope this is okay to say, but... I don't know if I'd say one is worse than the other, really. I try so hard to keep both things under control, I promise I do. I just want to be good and do what I'm supposed to. \n\nI know I said my mind slips off sometimes, and I feel just terrible about that... but the moving around, well, I guess that's just me trying to be helpful and stay busy, you know? I don't want to be a bother to anybody with either of those things. I'm not really sure which one is a bigger problem... I hope it's alright that I don't have a better answer for you. Am I doing okay with these questions?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Well, um... I feel like you might have just asked me something similar, and I really hope I didn't give a bad answer the first time. I just... I don't really think the attention stuff is worse than the moving around, or the other way around.

It's probably nothing too bad, but whenever I have to sit still—like, you know, at church, or out there in the clinic waiting room, or even just sitting at home—I start feeling really trapped. And I just have to get up or do something. \n\nI try really hard not to be a bother to anyone when it happens, I promise. Does that... is that the kind of thing you can help with?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to focus on things or pay attention when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I try my best to pay attention, I really do. I don't ever want to seem rude or like I'm ignoring anybody. But, um... I guess maybe a little bit? \n\nLike, when folks are talking through things with me—you know, like ride times, or pantry lists, or plans for church—I always make sure to listen real good at first. I'm right there with 'em. But then... I don't know, my mind just sort of slips off somewhere else. And I end up missing pieces I really needed to hear. \n\nI feel terrible about it, honestly, because I really am trying to be good and listen. I hope that makes sense... did I answer your question right?\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... act on impulse? Well, I don't think I do that too much. I really try to be careful and think about what I'm doing, especially around other people. I don't want to cause any trouble or make any sudden moves that might upset anyone. \n\nI mean, maybe sometimes I'll speak up a little too quick if I'm really excited about something, but I try real hard to keep that in check. I don't think I do anything too crazy without thinking. Is that... does that sound okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answers.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety?\n\nPatient: Well, I... Sometimes I don't even realize I'm doing it until I look down and see I'm twisting a piece of paper or my shirt hem in my hands the whole time. \n\nI'm so sorry if I'm doing it right now, I really don't mean to. It's just a little habit, you know? I hope that's not too bad of a thing to admit... is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: When did you first start feeling like you have a motor running inside you?\n\nPatient: A motor? Oh, well... yeah, I guess that is a real good way to put it. I hope it's okay to agree with that. I guess it's been about two years and two months now. \n\nIt started getting a lot more noticeable right around the time my older relative's health changed. I had to step up and take on a whole lot more responsibilities, which I don't mind at all—I really want to do a good job taking care of them and making sure everything gets done right. But ever since then, that... that motor feeling just won't seem to quit, no matter how hard I try to settle down and be still. \n\nI feel bad complaining about it, because I just want to be useful to them, you know? I'm trying so hard to do better, I promise I am. I hope I'm explaining that right... is that the kind of answer you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this restlessness and need to move around at work or school as well, or is it mainly in situations like church or at home?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I don't work right now, I'm just focusing on taking care of things at home. But, um, to answer your question... it's kind of everywhere, to be honest. I hope it's alright to say that. \n\nLike I was saying about that motor feeling, it just goes all day. So if I'm at home, or if I have to go to the clinic with my relative, or even if I'm sitting in church... I just start feeling really, um, trapped? Like I'm stuck and I just have to get up and do something, anything, just to get that energy out. \n\nI really try to hold it in, especially at church, because I don't want to be disrespectful or make anyone think I'm not paying attention. I just... I can't seem to help it sometimes. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding too difficult or anything.\n\nDoctor: Has this feeling of having a motor running inside you ever caused problems for you in your daily activities or relationships?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try hard to make sure my pacing and fidgeting doesn't bother anyone. I just want to be useful. But... I guess, if I'm being completely honest—and I hope it's okay to tell you this—it has caused some trouble. I feel so ashamed even saying it out loud. \n\nUm... the people at church, and down at the food pantry, and even my own family... they've sort of stopped trusting me with things. Like, they don't ask me to do rides anymore, or help keep track of the appointments, or handle the supplies. I just get too antsy and moving too fast, and things get messed up. \n\nI feel like I just keep letting everybody down, even when I really, truly mean to help them. I'm trying so hard to do better, I promise I am. I hope that doesn't make me sound like a bad person... did I answer your question okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble paying attention or following instructions at home, like when you're trying to take care of your relative or doing chores?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really do try my best to pay attention, I promise I do. I want to make sure I get everything exactly right for everybody. But... well, I guess sometimes things do slip a little bit. I hope it's okay to admit that. \n\nLike, when folks are talking through the ride times for my relative, or going over the pantry lists, or explaining the plans for church... I listen real good at first. I'm right there with them. But then, it's like... my mind just sort of slips off somewhere else, even though I don't want it to. And then I end up missing pieces of what they said, pieces I really needed to hear to do a good job. \n\nI feel awful when it happens, because I know they're counting on me. I really am trying to follow the instructions better. Does that... does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding too scattered.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're speaking?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try to be polite, I promise. I know it's bad manners to talk over folks, and I just want to show everyone I'm ready to help. But... well, I guess if I'm being completely honest... I do kind of do that sometimes. I hope you don't think I'm a rude person. \n\nLike, if the pastor, or my cousin, or the lady down at the food pantry is explaining something to me... I just get so eager, and I jump right in with an answer before they're even done talking. My brain just goes so fast, and the words just spill out before I can stop them. \n\nAnd then... well, then I realize I answered the completely wrong thing, or I see that I made them mad because I cut them off. I feel so awful when it happens, because I really don't mean any disrespect by it. I'm just trying to show them I'm on top of things. I hope I'm not making myself sound too terrible... did I explain that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you often blurting out answers before the questions have been completely asked?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I know I just mentioned jumping in sometimes when folks are talking, but... I don't think I really blurt out answers to questions before they're finished asking. I haven't noticed myself doing that, anyway. I hope that's a good thing... am I making sense? Oh, um... well, I really hope this doesn't make me sound awful, because I promise I always mean well. I just... I want to be helpful, you know? But, um...

Oh, um... changes to my routine? I... I don't think so. I hope it's okay to say that, I just... I haven't really changed much on purpose.

I really do try my best to pay attention, I promise I do. I want to make sure I get everything exactly right for everybody. But... well, I guess sometimes things do slip a little bit. I hope it's okay to admit that. \n\nLike, when folks are talking through the ride times for my relative, or going over the pantry lists, or explaining the plans for church...
